## Candidate forms {#candidate-forms}

The yardsticks are: the smallest U10 that sustains soaring; progress speed over the ground at the design wind; survival on the water (self-righting, sealing, take-off); solar area; mass; what one person can build and repair; cost.
The candidates to fly in the environment are:

1. **Conventional glider with a tail boom**, the baseline of the structural model (span wing.span_m, wing area form.baseline_2m.wing_area_m2), like the demonstrators: the foam trainer of P48 (lit.easyglider_span_m) and a smaller demonstrator (form.demo_1p5m.span_m) that has no source in the record yet (Q-014). Known handling; the boom and tail are fragile on the water.
2. **Flying wing or tailless with winglets**, span form.flyingwing_2m.span_m. Fewer parts to break, easier to seal, but a lower aspect ratio (its wing area is form.flyingwing_2m.wing_area_m2) and less pitch control margin.
3. **Albatross-like high-aspect-ratio glider**, span form.albatross_3m.span_m, wing area form.albatross_3m.wing_area_m2, mass form.albatross_3m.mass_kg. Best lift-to-drag and lowest wind to soar; the biggest to launch and the most to lose.
4. **Sealed hull glider that floats hull-down**, span form.sealedhull_2p5m.span_m, with the fuselage as a watertight buoyancy body and the wing sealed as a foam-core shell. The form that takes R-05 and R-06 most seriously; take-off from the water is its open question.

The parameters of each candidate are the keys form.<candidate>.span_m, mass_kg, wing_area_m2, cd0, oswald and cl_max, taken from the aircraft registry in `sim/aircraft_models.py` (assumed, Q-014); the span and mass of the baseline are wing.span_m and wing.design_mass_kg.
The comparison is in the next section; availability and rough cost follow the demonstrators' parts (carbon tubes, foam, fiberglass, PX4-class autopilot).

## Form comparison {#form-comparison}

The candidates were flown in the environment by the collocation optimizer of periodic cycles (`sim/dsopt.py`, a point mass in a height-only wind over a flat sea).
The run now uses the corrections of K-007: the wingtip height is the geometric (b/2) sin|mu| cos gamma, and the roughness length is solved together with the minimum wind instead of being fixed at its 8 m/s value (Q-054).
The numbers are in `sim/out/dsopt_corrected.json`, whose meta block holds the call, the commit and the stamp of its inputs; the first run, at commit 86d6689 in `sim/out/dsopt.json`, is kept for comparison.
Candidate 1 is form `baseline_2m`, candidate 2 `flyingwing_2m`, candidate 3 `albatross_3m` and candidate 4 `sealedhull_2p5m`; `demo_1p5m` and `easyglider_1p8m` are the demonstrators as references.
For each candidate the record holds the smallest U10 that sustains a travelling cycle and a loiter cycle, with the log profile and with the swell knee (Q-011), as the keys form.<candidate>.u10_min_travel_log, u10_min_loiter_log, u10_min_travel_knee and u10_min_loiter_knee.
Where the travelling minimum is below the wind of the cross-wind case, the record also holds the progress speed across the wind at that wind, the objective of the case, as form.<candidate>.cross12_speed_log and cross12_speed_knee; it now exists for baseline_2m, albatross_3m and sealedhull_2p5m with both profiles.
The mean ground speed of those cycles is higher than the progress speed and is in the file.
Only converged cycles are recorded, and the cases that were skipped because the travelling minimum is above the cross-wind wind are left out.

What the keys show:

- The lowest minimum wind belongs to the albatross-like glider, form.albatross_3m.u10_min_travel_log; the conventional baseline and the sealed hull come next, and the flying wing and the two demonstrators need much more wind.
- The corrections lowered every minimum, by about a tenth for the baseline and less for the albatross-like glider, against the first run; the order of the candidates did not change.
- A loiter cycle needs more wind than a travelling cycle for every candidate, with either profile.
- The swell knee raises the minimum for four of the six candidates and lowers it slightly for the other two (the flying wing and the foam trainer).
- No candidate reaches ds.min_u10_ms, the design point of R-02; the best, form.albatross_3m.u10_min_travel_log, is the nearest.
- This is raised as finding Q-024, and the owner decides whether to lower the wing loading, change the form, or raise the design wind.

The roughness of the sea decides much of this.
The literature on dynamic soaring uses a fixed z0 of 0.03 m (P45, P86), which is about a hundred times the Charnock value at these winds and gives about twice the shear.
With that roughness the same run (`sim/out/dsopt_z0_003.json`) gives form.baseline_2m.u10_min_travel_log_z003 for the baseline and form.albatross_3m.u10_min_travel_log_z003 for the albatross-like glider; the keys form.<candidate>.*_z003 hold the rest.
Then the baseline, the albatross-like glider, the sealed hull and the small demonstrator come below ds.min_u10_ms, the flying wing is close to it, and only the foam trainer stays far above.
The literature's roughness turned out to be a land value (see the roughness of the open sea), so the sea values are the Charnock and COARE 3.5 ones, and with them no candidate reaches the design point of R-02 on a flat sea in neutral air.
With COARE 3.5 (`sim/out/dsopt_coare35.json`, keys form.<candidate>.*_coare35) the minimum winds move little: form.albatross_3m.u10_min_travel_log_coare35 for the albatross-like glider and form.baseline_2m.u10_min_travel_log_coare35 for the baseline; the small, draggy demonstrators gain most, since COARE 3.5 gives a rougher sea than the constant Charnock value at their higher winds.
That roughness is about dsopt.z003_over_charnock_ratio times the Charnock value of the sea at such a wind, so the low numbers are not sea values yet (K-009).
They also rest on the assumed lift-to-drag ratios of the candidates (Q-014): the minimum wind scales about as the inverse of the lift-to-drag ratio and hardly with the wing loading, since the albatross-like glider with its ratio lowered to that of the Sachs albatross needs dsopt.albatross3m_ld20_u10_ms, close to the heavier bird (K-009).

The same optimizer was run against P45: for an albatross model it gives dsopt.validation_u10_ms where P45 gives lit.min_u10_albatross_ms; without the wingtip clearance, which P45 does not impose, it gives dsopt.validation_no_tip_u10_ms (K-007).
The speed floor (a minimum airspeed set from the stall speed) was off for the validation run and on for the form study, so the two are not the same experiment.
The values table in docs/01-requirements.md lists every key named here.
The optimizer has had three independent checks, K-007 and K-009 on the flat cycles and K-008 on the wave mode; all found it sound as a solver, and K-009 reproduced this rerun to every digit.
They also found defects in its constraints and its search: the flat mode keeps the wingtip clear only at the nodes, and between them it dips to dsopt.flat_tip_mid_min_baseline_m for the baseline (Q-059).

## Cycles synchronized with the waves {#wave-cycles}

The flat-sea cycles of the form comparison ignore the waves; the owner asked that the flight be planned in the height above the moving surface and in the wind that goes with the waves (Q-057).
`sim/dsopt.py` has a wave mode for one dominant deep-water wave component:

- it measures the height above the local surface and uses the wind of `sim/seawind.py` for that component, the log profile over the local surface plus the wave-coherent wind;
- it keeps the height and the wingtip above the moving surface at the nodes and at the midpoints between them;
- the cycle repeats relative to the wave: after one period the wave has slipped by a whole number of wavelengths, and the optimizer also chooses where in the wave the cycle starts;
- options give the measured decay rates of the wave wind (P63) and the sheltering of `sim/seawind.py`;
- the module also has the corrections of K-007, the geometric wingtip height and the roughness length consistent with the wind found.

Command: `python -m sim.dsopt --wave-study2`, which writes `sim/out/dsopt_wave.json`.
Each case is one run of a local optimizer, so the results are examples, not a survey.

What it found:

- Over a dominant swell wave of the grid (Hs 1 to 3 m, Tp 6 to 11 s, along and across the wind), dsopt.wave_floor_cases of dsopt.wave_cases cases closed a cycle at the lowest wind allowed: the air moved by the waves alone carried the glider at least 1 m above the surface.
- Where the wind is needed, the low turn sits on the forward face of the wave or on its crest, where the wave pushes the air up, and never in the trough.
- The measured decay rates moved the minimum wind by at most dsopt.measured_decay_max_shift_ms.
- With sheltering, the albatross-like glider over a steep 20 m wave needed dsopt.shelter_albatross_l20_on_ms instead of dsopt.shelter_albatross_l20_off_ms; the cycle changed and its low point moved to the crest, not into the trough.

What the independent check found (K-008):

- The wave-only cycles are real solutions of the model: a replay in a separate model closes them and balances the energy.
- They are unstable, growing by up to dsopt.wave_cycle_floquet_max per cycle without feedback, so flying them needs the closed loop of Q-057.
- The count is not meaningful: the optimizer keeps the number of wavelengths slipped per cycle from its starting guess and stays in local optima, and a steady-ride estimate puts at least dsopt.wave_steady_ride_cases cases at the floor (Q-059).
- The physics behind them is weak: in calm air the model's vertical wave wind is dsopt.wave_vertical_ratio_model of the water's orbital speed, where the measurement over swell gives about dsopt.wave_vertical_ratio_measured (P64); with that ratio the two replayed cases need dsopt.wave_scaled_u10_baseline_tp8_hs2_ms and dsopt.wave_scaled_u10_albatross_tp6_hs1_ms of wind (Q-058).
- The steep-wave cases in calm air are artefacts, since their controls swing at rates no aircraft has and linear wave theory does not hold at that steepness; they are dropped.
- The sheltering does act where the wind outruns a steep wave; the reading that it hardly acts because of the onset slope was wrong, since in calm air it is the waves outrunning the air that shut it off.

What it means for the design:

- Swell can carry part of the energy, and with a calibrated wave wind the wind needed over swell may still fall below the flat-sea minimum; that is a route towards ds.min_u10_ms of R-02, and it waits for Q-058 and Q-059.
- The flight control must follow the wave phase with feedback, which is the receding-horizon planner of Q-057.

### Second pass: replay-checked cycles over swell (wave study 4)

The second pass (`python -m sim.dsopt --wave-study4`, `sim/out/dsopt_wave4.json`) fixed what K-008 and K-011 found and asked the question again with the calibrated wave wind:

- every cycle is replayed densely and counts only if it closes and keeps its height and wingtip between nodes; long cycles are re-solved on a finer mesh until they do;
- the wave wind uses the vertical amplitude and the decays of P63 (the horizontal amplitude stays 1.0 against P63's 0.95, which K-012 found changes nothing at the floor), and the low bound of P64 for the vertical part;
- roll and lift-coefficient rates, the load-factor floor and COARE 3.5 are in, and each case runs with the control margins (lowest point 2 m, 0.8 of the load limit) and without them;
- the phase is pinned on the wave and several phase slips and rotated starts are tried.

Of dsopt.wave4_cases_planned cases, dsopt.wave4_cases_run ran within the time; the rest (the albatross-like glider at the longer period and part of the low bound) are left for later.

What it found:

- Over a 3 m swell both candidates sustain a cycle with no wind at all, with and without the margins: dsopt.wave4_floor_cases cases in all, every case run with the wave wind of P63 and dsopt.wave4_p64_floor_cases of dsopt.wave4_p64_cases_run with the low bound of P64 (the one miss looks like a local optimum, since the same case along the wave reaches it); the baseline's cycles last dsopt.wave4_baseline_floor_period_min_s to dsopt.wave4_baseline_floor_period_max_s and turn low mostly on the forward face or the crest, in two cases on the rear face.
- They are wave-slope soaring: an independent replay (K-012) closes them, keeps their margins, and finds the energy coming from the updraft of the forward face, about 1.2 m/s at 2 m above a 3 m swell with P63 and 0.65 m/s with the low bound, against a sink of 0.40 m/s; one cycle rides a single wave at about the wave speed.
- Three of the albatross-like glider's seven cycles over 3 m swell sit at the period bound of dsopt.wave4_period_bound_s and are not natural periods, so for those their existence is shown but not their shape.
- Over a 1 m swell the waves do not help: only one case beats the flat sea, by dsopt.wave4_hs1_best_gain_ms, against flat minima of dsopt.wave4_flat_margin_baseline_ms and dsopt.wave4_flat_margin_albatross_ms with margins and dsopt.wave4_flat_none_baseline_ms and dsopt.wave4_flat_none_albatross_ms without; the others need more wind than the flat sea, because a cycle forced to repeat with the wave pays for that when the wave gives little, so for small waves the right answer is the better of a wave-synchronized and an unsynchronized cycle.

What it means: a large swell can carry the drone with no wind, if it follows the wave phase; a small swell cannot, and the wind must do it.
This is the bird's light-wind behaviour (P42, P74, P80), and it makes the wave-aware closed loop the next thing to show (Q-060); it is shown in the next section.

## Flight over swell in the closed loop {#wave-loop}

Open item Q-060 asks that the receding-horizon planner keep the drone in the air over waves and draw energy from them.
This pass does that in a first stage with a narrow scope:

- the planner knows the sea exactly (an oracle sea); estimating the sea from the sensors is the second stage (Q-064);
- the sea is long-crested swell;
- the aircraft are the albatross-like glider and the baseline;
- the wave-coherent wind is the calibration of P63, with the low bound of P64 beside it (Q-058);
- the margins are the library's standard set (lowest point 2 m above the local surface, the wingtip clearance of the library, 0.8 of the load limit, 0.9 of the bank limit, 1.2 stall speeds); the wingtip close to the water is left to Q-063;
- the reflex is off (Q-068).

The code is `sim/wavelib.py` with the changes to `sim/mpc.py` named below; the unit tests are `sim/tests/test_wavelib.py`.
The commands are `python -m sim.mpc --wave-diagnosis` (key `wave_diagnosis` of `sim/out/mpc.json`), `--build-wave-library` (`sim/out/mpc_library_wave.json`), `--wave-loop-exp` (key `wave_loop_exp`), `--wave-sense-exp` (key `wave_sense_exp`) and `--wave-nav-exp` (key `wave_nav_exp`), each stamped with the commit and the files it used; `python -m sim.wave_keys` writes the record keys of all of them and the key tables below.

### Why the earlier wave runs ended

The motor-library experiment of the receding-horizon section flew a fully developed wind sea at 13 and 16 m/s for 180 s, and some of its wave runs ended early.
Each of them was flown again with every plan logged (`--wave-diagnosis`); all wave.diag_runs reproduced their recorded end to the step (wave.diag_reproduced).

What the planner knew and followed:

- With the "flat" world (wave.diag_flat_world_runs of the runs) the rollouts saw a flat surface at the mean sea level and the log wind with no wave-coherent wind.
- With the "truth" world they saw the surface and the wave wind of the 48 strongest components, not the whole sea.
- The reference was the flat-sea motor library's cycle, with its height measured above the local surface (a terrain-following version of a flat cycle), and the wave-aware phase shift was off; nothing in the reference or the cost asked the planner to use the waves.

Where and why they ended:

- None ended on a stall or for lack of energy: the motor ran most of the time in every run (at least wave.diag_motor_share_min of it), so the waves were never the source of the energy.
- wave.diag_crest_tip_runs ended with a wingtip touching the top of a crest, where the surface stood wave.diag_crest_eta_std_min to wave.diag_crest_eta_std_max standard deviations above the mean; they are wave.diag_crest_tip_trajectories distinct trajectories, since the 13 m/s flat-world run with the reflex on never fired it and so flew the same path as the one without.
- In the flat-world runs that ended on a wingtip, the last plans predicted the wingtip wave.diag_flat_plan_tip_min_m to wave.diag_flat_plan_tip_max_m above their flat surface, while against the true surface the same plans put it at wave.diag_flat_true_tip_min_m to wave.diag_flat_true_tip_max_m, in the water: the planner did not see the crest.
- In the truth-world run that ended, the last plans predicted the wingtip wave.diag_truth_plan_tip_m clear of the 48-component surface but wave.diag_truth_true_tip_m against the whole sea, and on average wave.diag_truth_dead_mean of the 96 samples ended in contact: the shortened sea hid the surface there (the contact was near the mean level, wave.diag_truth_end_eta_std standard deviations above it, not on a crest).
- wave.diag_looped_runs runs had looped (the flight path angle had turned through a full circle) before they ended, and they are exactly the runs whose reflex had fired, the zoom past the vertical that K-015 found (Q-068).

The same planner over a regular 3 m swell of 10 s (wave.diag_swell_runs runs, calm and 6 m/s, with and without the old phase shift) flew all of them to the end (wave.diag_swell_complete), but with the motor on all the time (wave.diag_swell_motor_share_min), at wave.diag_swell_power_min_w to wave.diag_swell_power_max_w, wave.diag_swell_h_median_min_m to wave.diag_swell_h_median_max_m above the surface, and once as low as wave.diag_swell_h_min_m: it terrain-follows its flat cycle and takes nothing from the swell.

So the planner needed three things: the true surface in its rollouts (the oracle sea of this stage), a reference that is synchronized with the waves, and a guide that keeps that reference on the right part of the wave.

### The sea of the experiments

`swell_env` builds the sea of the optimizer's wave mode inside `sim/seawind.py`: one regular long-crested deep-water component of amplitude Hs / 2 and period Tp, the COARE 3.5 log wind over the local surface, and the wave-coherent wind of the preset (P63: vertical amplitude lit.wave_induced_amp_w and the decays lit.wave_induced_decay_u and lit.wave_induced_decay_w; the low bound of P64: the lower vertical amplitude that Q-058 sets for it, as in wave study 4), with the horizontal amplitude of the optimizer's presets (a new opt-in keyword `wave_amp` of `Environment`).
A unit test requires the wind of this sea to equal the optimizer's wave wind to rounding; the surface for contact and for the height above the surface is the second-order one of the simulator-fitness section.
Calm is flown at the wave mode's lower bound of the wind, 0.1 m/s.
A regular wave has no wave groups; an irregular long-crested swell is the next sea to fly.

### The wave library

`--build-wave-library` writes two kinds of entries, each with its sea (aircraft, preset, Hs, Tp, the swell's direction relative to the wind, wind), its course relative to the swell, the wave phase at its start, its mean ground velocity, its average electric power (zero for a soaring entry) and its replay result.

Cycles of the optimizer (`solve_wave_entry`): for each course a soaring cycle of the wave mode, the speed along the course maximised, with the folded propeller over the whole cycle, the standard margins, COARE 3.5, the geometric wingtip, the midpoints, the roll and lift-coefficient rates, n >= 0, the phase pinned on the wave, three phase slips around each start, and the dense replay check with mesh refinement (Q-059); where no soaring cycle passes, a motor cycle of least average power with a mean speed along the course of at least half the best-glide speed.
The grid is Hs 3, 2 and 1.5 m, Tp 8, 10 and 12 s, the swell along, across and against the wind, u10 0, 3, 6 and 9 m/s, and courses of 0, 90 and 180 deg from the swell's travel (and -90 deg for the swell across the wind, where the two sides are not mirror images).
The optimizer's cycles were solved only at Tp 10 s for Hs 3 and 2 m (the part kept complete); a slot costs about half a minute to four minutes of one core, so the rest of the grid has the crest rides only.

Crest rides (`crest_ride`, `crest_entries`): steady flight in the wave frame on the forward face, the pelican's wave-slope soaring.
The aircraft moves with the swell at the phase speed and along the crest at a chosen speed, wings level, so its phase and its height above the local surface stay constant and the wind it meets is constant; a ride exists where the forward face's updraft balances the sink at the airspeed this needs.
At each along-crest speed the rides form a family over the height: at every height the phase on the crest side of the face where the updraft balances the sink, up to 10 m or the balance height at the phase of the largest surplus.
A crest ride is not stable on its own: with the controls held, a small airspeed error makes the aircraft drift slowly forward or back over the face and away from the ride's height on either side of the surplus maximum (K-020), so the ride relies on the planner's feedback; the crest side was kept because the pilot flights reached it from below.
Each sea keeps the ride with the slowest along-crest speed that soars (the course nearest the swell's travel) and the fastest either way; the speeds are searched on a 0.5 m/s grid and a speed counts only where the ride itself is found there (the first build tried only three screened speeds and lost the slowest soaring one in wave.lib_crest_rebuild_changed_seas seas, among them the 3 m swell along a 6 m/s wind; `--rebuild-crest-rides` rebuilt the crest part of the library, K-020). Where none soars, a sea keeps the steady ride with the motor of least electric power, at 2 m.
A replay in the optimizer's dynamics started exactly on each soaring ride stays on it over a crest period; this shows that the ride is an equilibrium, not that it is stable.

### The planner over waves

The rollouts already fly the planner's world; with the oracle sea that world is the true sea, so the height and the wingtip are measured against the moving second-order surface and the rollouts meet the wave wind.
What changed (`sim/mpc.py`):

- A library entry may carry the wave phase at the aircraft per node; `Cycle.theta_at` continues it over the periods.
- `WaveCycleGuide` replaces `CycleGuide` for such an entry: the match picks the cycle phase that best fits the height above the local surface, the airspeed, the flight path angle, the heading relative to the wind and the wave phase at the aircraft, so the time shift is the one that puts the cycle's wave phase on the sea's; the terminal cost and a running tracking cost (weight 5 per second) add the wave-phase error, so the plans keep the aircraft on the same part of the wave as the cycle.
- For a crest ride the guide follows the member of its family 2 m above the aircraft's present height, so the reference is always reachable and leads the aircraft upwards where the face has a surplus; the airspeed tolerance is loose (5 m/s) and the phase tolerance tight (0.1 rad), since the speed must float to hold the phase.
- The wave library comes in through `fly_mpc(wave_library=...)` (`WavePilot`): it reads the swell of the planner's world, matches the library's nearest sea and wind (a sea with the swell to the right of the wind flies the mirrored entries), and names an entry; a run starts in flat flight (the steady motor leg of the navigation section at 10 m, `start_entry`), and the change onto a wave entry, or between wave entries, waits until the wave phase at the aircraft is within 0.3 rad of the cycle's (at most 60 s), as the soaring tacks wait for theirs.
- The pilot's policy "energy" takes soaring entries first (crest rides first, the slowest along-crest speed), else the entry of least power, and stays on the start's flat flight when that entry needs at least the flat library's least power at the wind.
- The wave runs sample with three smooth perturbation modes; without them the planner could not find the coherent bank that turns onto a ride's heading, and it took minutes to turn.
- Without a wave library nothing changes; with one over a sea without a swell nothing changes but the first plan, which starts from the cycle's own controls and thrust whether the world has a swell or not, so that a sea still being estimated starts the same way (a unit test requires the same trajectory to the bit as a run without the library seeded alike); the viewer flies the same wave loop in its swell setting (see the viewer section).

Found on the way, with short pilot flights over a 3 m swell of 10 s (not recorded):

- Taking the wave entry at the first plan, without waiting for the phase, locked the aircraft onto the rear face, where it held the wave speed with the motor running.
- In the short pilot flights the ride at 20 m was not reached from below: the plans held a compromise under it and slid back over the crest. So the family's top was set at 10 m; this is a choice, not a limit of the sea, since rides soar higher in the model (K-020).
- With the tight airspeed and loose phase tolerances of the cycles the aircraft drifted slowly backwards over the crest, each plan predicting the slip but paying more for a speed error than for the phase; the crest tolerances above hold it.
- A heavy tracking weight (20 per second, terminal 50) dived the aircraft into the water at the start.

### Closed loop over swell

`--wave-loop-exp`: both aircraft over a regular swell of Hs 3 and 2 m at Tp 10 s, the swell along and against the wind, u10 0, 3, 6 and 9 m/s, with the wave wind of P63 and the low bound of P64, each for 10 minutes or until the run ends; calm runs once and stands for both directions.
The oracle sea, the truth state and wind, the motor allowed at the middle energy weight (its cost in the planner), the actuators, the load-factor limit, the stop on a wingtip contact and no reflex, as the navigation runs; the start in flat flight along the swell's travel.
A run counts when it flew the whole time inside the band of R-12 (heights above the local surface) and the margins of the navigation section (`flight_margins`).
The power is the average electric power over the run (the same number as the energy per hour in Wh/h), and also from the entry onto the wave library on; the flat sea's figure is the flat motor library's least average power at the same wind (any course).

What the runs show:

- wave.loop_full of wave.loop_runs runs flew the whole ten minutes, and wave.loop_counted of them count (inside the band of R-12 and the margins), wave.loop_counted_p63 with the wave wind of P63 and wave.loop_counted_p64 with the low bound of P64.
- Every cycle the optimizer found for these seas needs no power but one: its motor cycles have zero thrust except wave.lib_optimizer_motor_needing_power, which needs wave.lib_optimizer_motor_power_max_w (the baseline over a 2 m swell across a 9 m/s wind with the low bound of P64). But wave.loop_cases_motor_crest_only of the wave.loop_runs cases have no soaring entry at all, only a motor crest ride, so the library says that the swell carries the aircraft only in the rest; what the runs measure is what it costs to fly the library in a closed loop.
- Where the swell carries the aircraft, the average electric power after the entry is wave.loop_ratio_min to wave.loop_ratio_max times the flat motor library's least nominal (open-loop) power at the same wind, with a median of wave.loop_ratio_median over the runs that count; the lowest power is wave.loop_power_min_w and the median wave.loop_power_median_w, against motor.albatross_flapglide_w and motor.baseline_flapglide_w for a steady flap-glide. The comparison favours the flat sea: the flat figure is the optimizer's, while the same planner flying the flat sea at 6 m/s paid mpc.burst_albatross_u6_normal_w.
- The motor never stops: its share of the time is wave.loop_motor_share_min at best and wave.loop_motor_share_median in the middle, and wave.loop_low_motor_runs runs that count keep it under a tenth of the time. Part of that is the planner's own floor (the clipped nominal thrust of the navigation section applies nav.thrust_bias_raw_basic_applied_pct % of the weight when thrust does not matter), and part is that a wave cycle flown with feedback is not the optimizer's cycle.
- The wind of 3 m/s is the worst case, not the best: wave.loop_u3_dearer_runs runs there cost more than the flat library, up to wave.loop_u3_power_max_w, because the loop loses the forward face. In wave.loop_u3_dearer_v_c0_runs of them the entry was the crest ride with no along-crest speed, whose family reaches the 10 m top, and the aircraft fell back behind the crest and held the wave's speed with the motor: in the wave.loop_u3_dearer_full_runs that flew the whole time it was on the forward face only wave.loop_u3_dearer_full_forward_face_min to wave.loop_u3_dearer_full_forward_face_max of the time after the entry (against a median of wave.loop_forward_face_median_counted in the runs that count at the other winds), with the motor on at least wave.loop_u3_dearer_full_motor_share_min of it. This is the failure the pilot flights showed for an entry off phase. The pilot chooses by the library's nominal power, which is zero for every soaring entry, so it cannot see that; what the loop pays would have to enter the choice.
- The two aircraft behave alike, and the low bound of P64 moves the picture rather than removing it: it ends more runs early and makes the motor work harder, but it also carries runs that the P63 wave wind loses (the albatross-like glider over a 3 m swell against a 6 and a 9 m/s wind).
- In wave.loop_flat_only runs the pilot refused the wave entry, because the only entry of that sea needed at least as much power as the flat library, and flew on the steady flat leg; wave.loop_flat_only_ended of them touched the water with a wingtip. A steady leg level above the local surface is not safe over a swell: its reference rides up and down with the surface, and the planner follows it into the troughs.

### The pilot's choice and the forward face (Q-060)

The runs above at 3 m/s lost the forward face, and the pilot could not see it because it chose by the library's nominal power.
Pilot flights of the albatross-like glider over the 3 m swell at 3 m/s with the crest ride of no along-crest speed forced (not recorded) showed three ways the loop pays:

- With the swell along the wind the flat leg nearly keeps pace with the swell, so the wave phase at the aircraft drifts slowly and the wait for the phase match can run out; the aircraft then reaches the ride's phase low and slow, slides back over the crest and holds the wave's speed behind it with the motor.
  The ride sits near the crest, on the side where a lag loses the updraft, and the planner did not spend the speed to stay ahead.
- With the swell against the wind the ride needs an airspeed far above the flat leg's, so the aircraft cannot get onto it and falls behind the crest in the same way.
- The family member above the aircraft (the guide's climb of 2 m) asks for height while the aircraft is behind the ride's phase, which costs the speed that would hold the phase.

The forward-face guide (`PILOT_FACE`, the face option of `WaveCycleGuide`, crest rides only):

- the wave-phase error is asymmetric: ahead of the ride's phase, further down the forward face where the updraft is stronger, it is tolerated with wave.pilot_face_sig_ahead_rad, behind it with the guide's tolerance as before;
- the member of the family is not taken above the aircraft while it is behind the phase of the ride at its own height;
- a bounded barrier on the phase at the aircraft, weight wave.pilot_face_barrier_w per second in the running cost: zero more than wave.pilot_face_barrier_m_rad down the face from the crest, rising quadratically to one at the crest, one behind the crest for a quarter wavelength, then falling linearly to zero at the trough behind (where the next wave's face is nearer); it pulls the aircraft back down the face before it slides over the crest, and being bounded it never outweighs the cost of a contact.

In the pilot flights the asymmetric tolerance and the member rule alone delayed the slide but did not stop it, a quarter of the barrier's weight did not stop it either, and a phase tolerance behind the ride tightened to half instead of the barrier held the face along the wind but dived the aircraft into the water against it, because the phase cost then outweighed a contact; with the barrier the aircraft held the face both ways, along the wind with the motor on part of the time and against it with the motor on most of the time, since it held a phase behind the ride and lower than it.

The guide's own effect over the experiment's seas (`--wave-pilot-noface`, key `wave_pilot_trials_noface`: every crest ride's trial below flown again without the guide, the same start, seed and flat leg): of wave.pilot_face_trials trials, wave.pilot_face_ended ended on the water with the guide against wave.pilot_noface_ended without it, wave.pilot_face_broke broke a margin against wave.pilot_noface_broke, and wave.pilot_face_kept kept the margins against wave.pilot_noface_kept; the guide lowered the cost of wave.pilot_face_lower trials and raised it in wave.pilot_face_higher, and over the wave.pilot_face_kept_both rides that kept the margins both ways the median cost is wave.pilot_face_kept_both_median_w W with it against wave.pilot_noface_kept_both_median_w W without.
So the guide keeps some rides off the water (wave.pilot_face_saved), but all but wave.pilot_face_saved_valid of them still break a margin; it changes none of the pilot's choices in the runs below (wave.pilot_face_choices_changed); and on the rides that hold both ways it lowers the cost in most pairs (wave.pilot_face_pairs_lower lower, wave.pilot_face_pairs_higher higher, a median change of wave.pilot_face_pairs_median_change_w W; the medians above compare two sets, not pairs; K-022); the gain at 3 m/s comes from the choice below, which leaves the rides that neither way can hold.

The pilot's choice by trial (`WavePilot(policy="trial")`, `pilot_trial_job`): before a run, every entry of the library's nearest sea (one of each mirror pair, since an entry and its mirror image cost the same from a start along the swell's travel) and the flat leg are each flown in a closed-loop trial of wave.pilot_trial_s from the run's start, in the planner's world (the oracle sea of this stage), with the same planner, guide and flat leg as the run but another planner seed; the cost of a trial is what the loop paid: the average electric power after the entry (over the whole trial for the flat leg), with a penalty when a margin broke or the band was left, a larger one when the entry was not taken, and a larger one still when the trial ended on the water; the run flies the entry of least cost, or stays on the flat leg.
For a sea that is estimated, whose direction wanders about the wind's, `WavePilot(mirror_hyst_deg=...)` keeps the side of a mirrored entry until the swell's direction or the course passes the axis by that much (`MIRROR_HYST_DEG`); it is off in these runs, whose sea lies on the axis.
This is a choice by simulation in the aircraft's own model of the sea: it costs one short flight per entry before each choice, which the simulation's clock does not count (Q-065), and in stage 2 its worth rests on the estimated sea.
In this stage the trial flies the simulator's own aircraft, wind and sea from the run's exact start with another planner seed, so it replays the run's first two minutes; `pilot_trial_job` cannot yet fly a planner's sea that differs from the truth, which stage 2 needs (K-022).
The flat leg of these runs is the powered leg above the mean sea level with the heading hold (see the powered leg below).
Flown along the swell's travel it does not hold its height, and wave.pilot_stall_on_flat_leg_runs of the runs that do not count broke the stall margin on it (K-022).

`--wave-pilot-exp` flies the trials and then all wave.pilot_runs cases of the closed-loop experiment with the entry of least cost, seed 1 as before, for 10 minutes (key `wave_pilot_exp`, the trials under `wave_pilot_trials`).

What the runs show, against the runs above (the earlier pilot, the same cases, seed and settings but the energy policy, no face guide and the flat leg above the local surface):

- All wave.pilot_runs runs flew the whole ten minutes (wave.pilot_full, against wave.loop_full) and wave.pilot_counted count (against wave.loop_counted), wave.pilot_counted_p63 with the wave wind of P63 and wave.pilot_counted_p64 with the low bound of P64 (against wave.loop_counted_p63 and wave.loop_counted_p64); wave.pilot_gained runs count that did not, wave.pilot_lost counts no more, and no run ends early where it did not before (wave.pilot_ended_now_not_before).
- The wind of 3 m/s: the highest power is now wave.pilot_u3_power_max_w W (against wave.loop_u3_power_max_w), the median wave.pilot_u3_power_median_w W against wave.pilot_u3_earlier_power_median_w W, wave.pilot_u3_counted of its wave.pilot_u3_runs runs count (against wave.pilot_u3_earlier_counted), and wave.pilot_u3_dearer_runs of them still cost more than the flat library.
  In most runs there the pilot left the crest rides for the optimizer's cycles (wave.pilot_u3_choice_cycle runs: the soaring cycle against the swell's travel and the motor cycles that need almost no thrust), while wave.pilot_u3_choice_crest baseline runs still fly a motor crest ride and wave.pilot_u3_choice_flat the flat leg; the cycles' trials beat the rides, several of which ended on the water or broke a margin in their own trials.
- The runs that ended on a wingtip before (wave.pilot_tip_cases): wave.pilot_tip_cases_complete of them fly the whole time now and wave.pilot_tip_cases_counted count.
- The pilot chose a crest ride in wave.pilot_choice_crest runs, an optimizer's cycle in wave.pilot_choice_cycle and the flat leg in wave.pilot_flat_choice (none of which ended, wave.pilot_flat_choice_ended, where wave.loop_flat_only_ended of the earlier flat-leg runs touched the water); wave.pilot_choice_same_as_earlier runs flew the same entry as before.
  Every crest ride chosen stayed on the forward face for the whole time after the entry (wave.pilot_crest_forward_face_min).
- Power: over all runs the median is wave.pilot_power_median_all_w W against wave.pilot_earlier_power_median_all_w W; wave.pilot_power_lower_runs runs need less than nine tenths of their earlier power and wave.pilot_power_higher_runs more than eleven tenths.
  Over the runs that count, the power after the entry is wave.pilot_ratio_min to wave.pilot_ratio_max of the flat library's least nominal power, median wave.pilot_ratio_median (against wave.loop_ratio_min, wave.loop_ratio_max and wave.loop_ratio_median), the lowest wave.pilot_power_min_w W and the median wave.pilot_power_median_w W (against wave.loop_power_min_w and wave.loop_power_median_w, over a larger and harder set of runs).
  The motor's share of the time is wave.pilot_motor_share_min at best and wave.pilot_motor_share_median in the middle, and wave.pilot_low_motor_runs runs that count keep it under a tenth (against wave.loop_motor_share_min, wave.loop_motor_share_median and wave.loop_low_motor_runs).
- The trials predict the power of the runs: the chosen trial's cost and the run's power after the entry differ by wave.pilot_trial_error_median_w W in the middle and wave.pilot_trial_error_max_w W at most, the spread of a closed loop between two planner seeds, and in many cases the two best trials lie within that spread (within 1 W in wave.pilot_choice_gap_below_1w cases, within 0.5 W in wave.pilot_choice_gap_below_0p5w).
  They do not predict the margins over ten minutes: every run that does not count kept the margins in its trial (wave.pilot_not_counted_valid_trial), and a guided crest ride that kept them for its trial slid over the crest soon after when flown on, at about wave.pilot_face_ride_slide_s s (K-022).
- Where the runs cost more: of the wave.pilot_power_higher_runs dearer runs, wave.pilot_power_higher_flat_runs fly the flat leg, chosen where every wave entry broke a margin or ended on the water in its trial (the baseline over the 3 m swell against a 9 m/s wind flew its soaring cycle before at wave.loop_baseline_hs3_against_u9_p63_power_w W but did not count, and now flies the flat leg at wave.pilot_baseline_hs3_against_u9_p63_power_w W and counts), and the highest ratio is one of them; the others fly the same motor crest ride as before or another cycle, after a longer start on the flat leg and at a power after the entry within the spread of the trials.
  The one run that no longer counts (the albatross-like glider over the 3 m swell in calm air) was below the stall speed for wave.pilot_albatross_hs3_calm_u0_p63_below_stall_s s on its start leg above the mean sea level, from wave.pilot_lost_stall_start_s s, before it took at wave.pilot_lost_entry_s s the soaring cycle its trial rated best by less than the spread of the trials (K-022).
- The choice puts the margins first: an entry that broke a margin in its trial is not flown while another keeps them, whatever it costs; this is a choice of this pass, and it decides the dearer runs above.

### Navigation over swell

`--wave-nav-exp`: the albatross-like glider over a 3 m swell of 10 s running along a 6 m/s wind, to a target 5 km away, the velocity hull built from the wave library's entries at that sea and wind with their mirror images (the sea is symmetric about the wind axis), the hull navigator of the navigation section with its steady motor legs, and the planner flying what it names, the wave entries through the wave guide.
The run starts in flat flight towards the target and the prediction is the kinematic run on the same hull (`sail_hull`).
The target asked for lies across the swell's travel (here across the wind as well); a second run, added because every soaring point of this hull travels with the swell, puts the target along the swell's travel instead.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| across the swell | wave.nav_arrival_s_across | wave.nav_reached_across | wave.nav_counted_across | wave.nav_vmg_ms_across | wave.nav_first_leg_vmg_ms_across | wave.nav_hull_vmg_ms_across | wave.nav_hull_s_across | wave.nav_energy_wh_across | wave.nav_motor_share_across | wave.nav_height_median_m_across | wave.nav_height_min_m_across | wave.nav_height_max_m_across | wave.nav_wingtip_min_m_across | wave.nav_below_stall_s_across | wave.nav_hull_points_across |
| with the swell | wave.nav_arrival_s_with | wave.nav_reached_with | wave.nav_counted_with | wave.nav_vmg_ms_with | wave.nav_first_leg_vmg_ms_with | wave.nav_hull_vmg_ms_with | wave.nav_hull_s_with | wave.nav_energy_wh_with | wave.nav_motor_share_with | wave.nav_height_median_m_with | wave.nav_height_min_m_with | wave.nav_height_max_m_with | wave.nav_wingtip_min_m_with | wave.nav_below_stall_s_with | wave.nav_hull_points_with |
<!-- wave_nav_keys end -->

What the two runs show:

- With the target along the swell's travel the drone soars there and the run counts (wave.nav_counted_with): it arrives after wave.nav_arrival_s_with s at wave.nav_vmg_ms_with m/s made good, against the hull plan's first leg at wave.nav_first_leg_vmg_ms_with m/s and the kinematic run's wave.nav_hull_vmg_ms_with m/s and wave.nav_hull_s_with s, on soaring legs only, two crest rides (the slow ride first, then the fast one on the other side of the swell's travel), for wave.nav_energy_wh_with Wh with the motor on wave.nav_motor_share_with of the time, between wave.nav_height_min_m_with and wave.nav_height_max_m_with m above the local surface, with the wingtip at least wave.nav_wingtip_min_m_with m up and never below the stall speed (wave.nav_below_stall_s_with s).
  Before K-020 this run flew the hull of the first library, which had lost the slow crest ride of this sea; it then ended its last minute on the optimizer's cycle along the swell, where the airspeed fell below the stall speed well after the last change of cycle, and did not count (K-020).
- With the target across the swell's travel the hull has no soaring way there: every soaring point of this sea travels with the swell (the crest rides make good along the crest but drift east at the wave speed), so no pair of them averages to a velocity along the line, and the navigator falls back to a steady powered leg, as it does across the wind on a flat sea.
  That leg is not flyable over a 3 m swell (Q-073): the run never arrived (wave.nav_reached_across), it ended at wave.nav_vmg_ms_across m/s of speed made good, that is farther from the target than it started, and spent wave.nav_energy_wh_across Wh with the motor on wave.nav_motor_share_across of the time; it was below the stall speed for wave.nav_below_stall_s_across s, and its wingtip came down to wave.nav_wingtip_min_m_across m (both at every recorded step; the subsampled margins of the first pass missed the wingtip).
  The aircraft did not hold the leg's course: its heading lay wave.nav_heading_off_min_deg_across to wave.nav_heading_off_max_deg_across deg off the commanded bearing in every four-minute window, and it crossed the target's line wave.nav_crossing_offset_m_across m east of the target.
  The steady leg also holds a constant height above the local surface, so its reference rises and falls with the swell; the same leg ended wave.loop_flat_only_ended of the closed-loop runs above.
  A powered leg over waves needs its own reference (a constant height above the mean surface, or a wave-synchronized powered cycle); that is Q-073.

### The powered leg over swell (Q-073)

`--wave-leg-exp` flies the navigation over the same sea (a 3 m swell of 10 s along a 6 m/s wind, P63, the oracle sea) to a target 5 km away across the swell's travel (north, the leg runs along the crests), against it (west, upwind, the leg meets the crests head on) and with it (east, the soaring run, whose start is the leg), with three powered legs (`sim.wavelib.WAVE_LEGS`):

- "surface": the steady leg of the navigation section, 10 m above the local surface (the leg of the runs above);
- "surface hold": the same with a heading hold in the planner's nominal;
- "msl hold": 10 m above the highest crest of the sea (`crest_elevation`, the second-order crest), held above the mean sea level (the datum "msl" of `SteadyModel` and of the planner's steady reference), with the heading hold.

The heading hold (`heading_tau_s` of the steady reference, nav.steady_heading_tau_s) makes the planner's nominal bank so that the heading error at the plan time decays in that time, a coordinated turn that fades over the horizon; the samples then perturb a plan that already corrects the heading.
The runs counted as arrived on the same criteria as above; the track error is the angle between the ground track (the logged positions every 5 s) and the commanded bearing, the median of each four-minute window, which the heading fields of the runs above do not give, since they compare the heading through the air and so carry the crab angle of the wind triangle.

| against the swell | msl hold | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_arrival_s | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_reached | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_counted | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_vmg_ms | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_energy_wh | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_motor_share | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_height_median_m | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_height_min_m | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_wingtip_min_m | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_below_stall_s | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_track_off_max_deg | wave.leg_against_msl_hold_heading_off_max_deg |

| with the swell | msl hold | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_arrival_s | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_reached | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_counted | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_vmg_ms | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_energy_wh | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_motor_share | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_height_median_m | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_height_min_m | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_wingtip_min_m | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_below_stall_s | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_track_off_max_deg | wave.leg_with_msl_hold_heading_off_max_deg |
<!-- pilot_leg_keys end -->

Why the course was not held:

- Neither the wave wind's mean nor a mismatch of the planner's model: with the controls of the leg held, the heading oscillates by a few degrees with each wave and keeps its mean, and the planner's prediction of each next half second matched the flight (pilot flights, not recorded).
- Nor the cross-track guidance: it turned the commanded bearing back towards the line, to its limit of 35 deg, as designed, and the aircraft did not follow.
- The planner did not correct the heading: each plan restarts from the steady reference's own wings-level controls, and its single weighted update moves the bank by wave.leg_diag_bank_update_min_rad to wave.leg_diag_bank_update_max_rad rad, which the next plan discards; the tight heading tolerance and the constant-bank candidates, which inflate the spread of the costs and make the weights nearly uniform (an effective sample size of wave.leg_diag_ess_frac_min to wave.leg_diag_ess_frac_max of the samples while the heading error grew), add to it, but a temperature without those candidates (the ground track wave.leg_diag_robust_temperature_track_off_min_deg to wave.leg_diag_robust_temperature_track_off_max_deg deg off the line) or a planner without them (wave.leg_diag_no_banked_track_off_min_deg to wave.leg_diag_no_banked_track_off_max_deg deg) still lost the course, against wave.leg_diag_recorded_track_off_min_deg to wave.leg_diag_recorded_track_off_max_deg deg as recorded, while a nominal carried over from the previous plan held it within wave.leg_diag_carried_nominal_track_off_deg deg (K-022).
  Over a flat sea nothing pushes the heading off, so the nominal holds it; over a swell the wave wind turns it by a few degrees each wave, and the heading wandered to the side where the wave carried the aircraft.
  The weighting and the reset of the nominal are open (Q-076).
- The reference height did not cause it: the leg 10 m above the local surface flies the course once the heading is held (wave.leg_across_surface_hold_track_off_max_deg deg off at most across the swell, against wave.leg_across_surface_track_off_max_deg deg without the hold).

What the runs show:

- Across the swell's travel, with the heading hold, both legs arrive and count, in wave.leg_across_msl_hold_arrival_s s at wave.leg_across_msl_hold_vmg_ms m/s made good for wave.leg_across_msl_hold_energy_wh Wh above the mean sea level and in wave.leg_across_surface_hold_arrival_s s for wave.leg_across_surface_hold_energy_wh Wh above the local surface, against the kinematic run's wave.leg_across_msl_hold_hull_vmg_ms m/s; neither comes near the stall speed (wave.leg_across_msl_hold_below_stall_s s), and the lowest wingtip is wave.leg_across_msl_hold_wingtip_min_m m and wave.leg_across_surface_hold_wingtip_min_m m.
  The leg without the hold (the run of the section above) never arrived (wave.nav_reached_across).
- Against the swell's travel and upwind the legs with the hold arrive and count in wave.leg_against_msl_hold_arrival_s s (wave.leg_against_msl_hold_energy_wh Wh) and wave.leg_against_surface_hold_arrival_s s (wave.leg_against_surface_hold_energy_wh Wh), at wave.leg_against_msl_hold_vmg_ms m/s against the hull's wave.leg_against_msl_hold_hull_vmg_ms m/s, with the lowest wingtip at wave.leg_against_msl_hold_wingtip_min_m m and wave.leg_against_surface_hold_wingtip_min_m m; the leg without the hold ended with a wingtip in the water (wave.leg_against_surface_wingtip_min_m m) after flying wave.leg_against_surface_track_off_max_deg deg off its line.
- With the swell's travel the run soars as before (wave.leg_with_msl_hold_arrival_s s, wave.leg_with_msl_hold_energy_wh Wh); its leg is only the start.
- The choice: the leg above the mean sea level with the heading hold.
  The heading hold is what makes either leg flyable; the two datums need nearly the same energy (the keys above), while the leg above the mean sea level keeps more height under the wingtip across and against the swell, holds a reference that does not depend on the surface under the aircraft (it needs the height of the crests, not their phase, which matters once the sea comes from the sensors, Q-064); across and against the swell's travel it held its height, but flown along it, as the runs over swell start, it did not: there the wave's vertical air acts one way for tens of seconds, and the aircraft rode from wave.leg_msl_along_height_min_m to wave.leg_msl_along_height_max_m m against its reference of wave.leg_msl_ref_height_m m, its start legs sinking to wave.leg_msl_along_start_low_min_m to wave.leg_msl_along_start_low_max_m m (K-022).
- A wave-synchronized powered cycle was not built: across or against the swell's travel no wave can carry the aircraft, since a cycle locked to one wave travels with it at its phase speed, so it would make way only with the swell, where the soaring entries already do.
- The flights over swell of the pilot's section above start in flat flight on this leg and fall back on it where the pilot chooses no wave entry.
  Flown along the swell's travel it does not hold its height, and wave.pilot_stall_on_flat_leg_runs of the runs there that do not count broke the stall margin on it (K-022).

### What the planner needs to know of the sea (stage 2)

`--wave-sense-exp` flies one case of the closed loop (the albatross-like glider, 3 m swell of 10 s along a 6 m/s wind, P63, 5 minutes) with the planner's sea carrying an error while the aircraft flies the true one: the swell's phase shifted by a constant angle, so that the planner places the crests behind (positive) or ahead of (negative) where they are, or its height scaled by 0.8, 0.9 or 1.2.
This says what the sensing of stage 2 (Q-064) must deliver, in the quantities a sensor has to estimate.

| phase +0.15 rad | wave.sense_phase_0p15_aloft_s | wave.sense_phase_0p15_counted | wave.sense_phase_0p15_in_band | wave.sense_phase_0p15_below_stall_s | wave.sense_phase_0p15_wingtip_min_m | wave.sense_phase_0p15_power_after_w | wave.sense_phase_0p15_motor_share | wave.sense_phase_0p15_height_median_m |

| as it is | wave.sense_none_aloft_s | wave.sense_none_counted | wave.sense_none_in_band | wave.sense_none_below_stall_s | wave.sense_none_wingtip_min_m | wave.sense_none_power_after_w | wave.sense_none_motor_share | wave.sense_none_height_median_m |
<!-- wave_sense_keys end -->

What it needs:

- The wave phase to a few tenths of a radian, and more on one side than on the other. A positive shift puts the planner's crests behind the true ones, so the aircraft holds a phase nearer the crest than it thinks: at 0.15 and 0.3 rad the flight counts at wave.sense_phase_0p15_power_after_w and wave.sense_phase_0p3_power_after_w against wave.sense_none_power_after_w with the sea as it is, but the median height falls to wave.sense_phase_0p3_height_median_m m; at 0.6 rad it sinks out of the band (wave.sense_phase_0p6_in_band), to a median of wave.sense_phase_0p6_height_median_m m and a wingtip of wave.sense_phase_0p6_wingtip_min_m m, at wave.sense_phase_0p6_power_after_w, and at 1.2 rad it costs wave.sense_phase_1p2_power_after_w.
  A negative shift puts the aircraft further forward on the face, where the updraft is stronger: at -0.6 rad it counts (wave.sense_phase_m0p6_counted) and climbs to wave.sense_phase_m0p6_height_median_m m at wave.sense_phase_m0p6_power_after_w; shifts the other way were tried only at -0.6 rad.
  For the 156 m wavelength of a 10 s swell, 0.3 rad is about 7 m of position along the swell or half a second of time.
- The swell's height: an estimate a tenth low (wave.sense_height_0p9_counted, at wave.sense_height_0p9_power_after_w) and a fifth high (wave.sense_height_1p2_counted, at wave.sense_height_1p2_power_after_w) both count with the 3 m swell's entry. A fifth low is not a pure height error: the pilot matches the library's nearest swell, so the 2.4 m it believes flies the 2 m swell's crest ride, a slower ride along the crest, which is why it flies higher (wave.sense_height_0p8_height_median_m m) and cheaper (wave.sense_height_0p8_power_after_w); a height estimate further than a tenth low is untested.
- Against that, the fit of the few-component estimator is off by mpc.fit_surface_error_min_m to mpc.fit_surface_error_max_m in surface height today, which is the whole amplitude of a 3 m swell, so the sensing of stage 2 is not there yet (Q-064); a swell is however the easiest case to fit, being long, narrow in frequency and visible over the whole track.

### What failed, and what remains

What failed in this pass:

- The planner never flies a wave entry with the motor quite off: the lowest share of the time with thrust is wave.loop_motor_share_min, where the optimizer's cycles and the crest rides need none at all. The clipped nominal thrust of the planner (see the navigation section) is one cause and is known; the other is the gap between a cycle solved open-loop and the same cycle flown with feedback, which the margins and the actuators widen.
- At 3 m/s of wind the pilot's choice of entry costs more than flying the flat sea: the loop loses the forward face and holds the wave's speed behind the crest with the motor, and the pilot cannot see that, because it chooses by the library's nominal power and every soaring entry has zero. The choice needs a cost that reflects what the loop pays, measured or estimated.
  The pilot's choice by trial and the forward-face guide above answer this (Q-060).
- In the pilot flights the ride at 20 m was not reached from below, so the family's top was set at 10 m; this is a choice, not a limit of the sea: rides soar higher (K-020), and the closed loop does climb above the top when the face gives a surplus (the sensing runs below).
- The steady powered leg, which the navigation layer falls back on, is not safe or useful over a swell (Q-073): it holds a constant height above the local surface, so its reference rides with the surface, and over a 2 to 3 m swell it touched the water in wave.loop_flat_only_ended of the closed-loop runs and lost the navigation run across the swell.
  The powered leg above answers this: the course was lost to the planner's heading, and the leg with the heading hold above the mean sea level arrives across and against the swell's travel (Q-073).
- The optimizer did not converge in part of its slots (each aircraft and wave wind has 33 of them per sea, three or four courses at each of ten winds and directions): wave.lib_albatross_p63_hs3_tp10_missing and wave.lib_baseline_p63_hs3_tp10_missing with the P63 wave wind over the 3 m swell, and wave.lib_albatross_p64_hs3_tp10_missing and wave.lib_baseline_p64_hs3_tp10_missing with the low bound, and more over the 2 m swell (the table above); the failures gather on the baseline with the low bound and at the highest wind of the grid, where the cycle is nearest the flat-sea one, and those seas keep their crest rides only.

The independent check K-020 (partial) reproduced every rerun to the bit and confirmed the energy balance of the rides, the closure of the optimizer's cycles and the closed-loop energy; it refuted the stability of the crest side, the choice of the slowest along-crest speed, the 10 m top as a limit, the reading of the 3 m/s runs, part of the navigation and sensing prose, and the subsampled margins. The code was fixed (`crest_entries`, `flight_margins`, `wave_keys`), the crest rides rebuilt, the closed-loop, sensing and navigation experiments rerun, and this section corrected.

What remains, in order:

- The sea from the sensors instead of the oracle (Q-064), with the accuracy above as the target; the first pass is the next section (docs/02-form.md#sea-estimation).
- The trials of the pilot from a sea that comes from the sensors, and at a cost an aircraft can carry (one short flight per entry, Q-065).
- The crest rides that end on the water or break a margin in their trials, at every wind, and a motor that never quite stops.
- The redesigned reflex (Q-068) against a sea that comes from the sensors, where its range trigger is armed.
- Irregular long-crested swell (wave groups), then a swell with a wind sea on top; a regular wave has no groups, and the library is indexed by Hs and Tp alone.
- The optimizer's wave cycles for the rest of the grid, and the slots that did not converge.
- The wingtip near the water (Q-063) and the crest rides at the heights a bird uses, which this pass kept at 2 m and above.

## Estimating the swell from the aircraft's own sensors {#sea-estimation}

Stage 2 of the flight over swell (Q-064) replaces the oracle sea of the closed loop by the sea the aircraft estimates from its own sensors.
The target is what the sensing runs of the closed loop asked for (see what the planner needs to know of the sea): the swell's phase to a few tenths of a radian, tighter on the side that puts the planner's crests behind the true ones (wave.sense_phase_0p3_counted against wave.sense_phase_0p6_in_band), and its height to about a tenth low and a fifth high (wave.sense_height_0p9_counted, wave.sense_height_1p2_counted).
The few-component fit of the receding-horizon section (`sim/seasense.py`) is off by mpc.fit_surface_error_min_m to mpc.fit_surface_error_max_m in surface height, so this pass builds an estimator aimed at the dominant swell instead.

The code is `sim/swellest.py`; the unit tests are `sim/tests/test_swellest.py`.
`python -m sim.swellest --errors` runs the open-loop error experiment (key `errors` of `sim/out/swellest.json`), `--oracle-paths` keeps the oracle closed loop's trajectories for it (`sim/cache/`, not committed), and `--loop` runs the closed loop with the estimated sea (key `loop`), each stamped with the commit and the files it used; `python -m sim.swellest_keys` writes the record keys and the key tables below.

### The sensors

Each sensor is a model with the values of the record (keys sense.*; the assumed ones wait on Q-074).

- A laser altimeter at the fuselage, of the LW20/C kind (M14): noise sense.lidar_noise_m, mass sense.lidar_mass_g, power sense.lidar_power_w (its current at 5 V), and an assumed rate sense.lidar_rate_hz and latency sense.lidar_latency_s (the latter M14's figure for the SF11, not for the LW20).
  Over water it returns at nadir only sense.lidar_nadir_return of the time (P85), and off nadir the return falls as the specular law of P135, exp(-tan^2(tilt) / s^2), with the mean square slope s^2 of P135 (sense.mss_low_coef below 7 m/s, sense.mss_cm_a and sense.mss_cm_b above), taken here as the probability of a return.
- A radar altimeter at the fuselage, the US-D1 (M11): noise sense.radar_noise_m (its precision above 1 m), rate sense.radar_rate_hz, mass sense.radar_mass_g, power sense.radar_power_w, and an assumed latency sense.radar_latency_s.
  It returns the surface point nearest to it inside a cone of half angle sense.radar_half_beam_deg (half its narrower field of view), found among eight rays; the estimator does not know which point returned and assumes the ray of the cone nearest the vertical, which is the footprint error of P137.
- A radar at each wingtip: the tip sensor of D-019, with the reach of the A121 with a lens (sense.tip_radar_rmax_m, M16), the noise of a 77 GHz radar over a small stream (sense.tip_radar_noise_m, assumed from P136; no source gives the A121's noise over the sea), half its narrower beam width (sense.tip_radar_half_beam_deg, M16) and its measuring current at 1.8 V (sense.tip_radar_power_w); its rate, latency and mass are assumed.
  For the skim the tip sensor needs to read only skid.req_range_span_m; to serve the estimate it would also have to reach the water from the heights of the crest rides and the climbs above them, which the model assumes with the reach of the chip with a lens and the beam of the bare chip (M16), a combination no source gives, and which is a proposal for the owner and not a requirement yet (Q-075).
  The tip radars look body-down; a sensor canted for the skim bank sees the water with the wings level only if its beam is wider than the cant.
- A forward-looking radar, sampling eight points 10 to 40 m ahead along the heading, with every value assumed (Q-074).
  A forward lidar is not modelled: from the heights of the rides the points ahead lie far off nadir, where the specular law of P135 leaves no return, and a drone lidar over the sea got returns only near nadir (P134).
- The navigation: a vertical and two horizontal Kalman filters (position, velocity, accelerometer bias) on a MEMS IMU, GNSS position and velocity and, in the sets that name it, a barometer.
  The IMU has the accelerometer noise sense.imu_accel_noise_ug (M21) and an assumed in-flight vertical acceleration error that includes the attitude's share (sense.imu_vbias_sigma_ms2 over sense.imu_vbias_tau_s).
  The GNSS receiver (M20) gives the horizontal position to sense.gnss_cep_m without corrections and the velocity to sense.gnss_vel_acc_ms at 50 %, taken as sense.gnss_vh_sigma_ms; the vertical error without corrections (sense.gnss_vert_sigma_m over sense.gnss_bias_tau_s), its white part, the vertical velocity error (sense.gnss_vz_sigma_ms over sense.gnss_vel_tau_s) and the rate are assumed, and so is the vertical error with RTK (sense.rtk_vert_sigma_m, from M20's figure with corrections).
  The barometer (M22) has the precision sense.baro_precision_m and the relative accuracy sense.baro_relative_m, taken as a drift over sense.baro_drift_tau_s, with an assumed static-source error in steady flight (sense.baro_static_sigma_m).
  The error of a linear filter does not depend on the motion, so each run draws its navigation error once.
  The estimator works in the navigation frame; the planner, which the simulation hands the true state, gets the estimated sea moved into the true frame by the navigation error at the time of the plan.
- The bank error of the attitude estimate (sense.att_sigma_deg over sense.att_tau_s, assumed) moves the tips' assumed heights by half the span times its cosine, so the tips' difference measures the slope of the sea only as well as the bank is known; the estimator carries the bank error as a state.
- A barometer over swell reads the swell's own air pressure: the linearized Bernoulli of the model's wave-coherent air flow (P63) gives low pressure over the crests, which the barometer reads as height.
  The vertical filter passes the part of it that falls where it trusts the barometer, and the estimator does not model it.
- RTK needs a base station within M20's longest baseline (sense.rtk_baseline_max_km), which the open sea does not have; within its service area PPP-RTK (CLAS) gives sense.clas_vert_cep_m vertically (M20), which this pass does not model.

<!-- swellest_sets begin -->
| set | range sensors | navigation | mass g | power W |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| lidar | laser altimeter | GNSS without corrections, IMU | est.set_lidar_mass_g | est.set_lidar_power_w |
| radar | radar altimeter | GNSS without corrections, IMU | est.set_radar_mass_g | est.set_radar_power_w |
| radar_tips | radar altimeter, two tip radars | GNSS without corrections, IMU | est.set_radar_tips_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_power_w |
| radar_tips_baro | radar altimeter, two tip radars | GNSS without corrections, IMU, barometer | est.set_radar_tips_baro_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_baro_power_w |
| radar_tips_rtk | radar altimeter, two tip radars | RTK GNSS, IMU, barometer | est.set_radar_tips_rtk_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_rtk_power_w |
| lidar_rtk | laser altimeter | RTK GNSS, IMU, barometer | est.set_lidar_rtk_mass_g | est.set_lidar_rtk_power_w |
| radar_tips_fwd_baro | radar altimeter, two tip radars, forward radar | GNSS without corrections, IMU, barometer | est.set_radar_tips_fwd_baro_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_fwd_baro_power_w |
| radar_tips_vwhite | radar altimeter, two tip radars | the same, white GNSS velocity error | est.set_radar_tips_vwhite_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_vwhite_power_w |
| radar_tips_fwd_rtk | radar altimeter, two tip radars, forward radar | RTK GNSS, IMU, barometer | est.set_radar_tips_fwd_rtk_mass_g | est.set_radar_tips_fwd_rtk_power_w |
| tips_rtk | two tip radars | RTK GNSS, IMU, barometer | est.set_tips_rtk_mass_g | est.set_tips_rtk_power_w |
<!-- swellest_sets end -->

The set radar_tips_vwhite is a sensitivity case: the sensors of radar_tips with a white GNSS velocity error (sense.gnss_vel_tau_white_s instead of sense.gnss_vel_tau_s).
The calculated sensor values: sense.lidar_power_w is M14's current at 5 V; sense.radar_half_beam_deg is half M11's narrower field of view and sense.tip_radar_half_beam_deg half M16's E-plane beam width; sense.tip_radar_power_w is M16's measuring current at its 1.8 V supply; sense.gnss_power_w is M20's tracking current at a 3.0 V supply and sense.imu_power_w M21's low-noise current at 1.8 V, both supplies assumed; sense.gnss_vh_sigma_ms is M20's 50 % velocity figure as the median of a normal error, divided by 0.674.
The masses and powers of the sets add the range sensors to the navigation sensors (GNSS, IMU, barometer where named, and for RTK its correction link).

### The estimator

`SwellEKF` is an extended Kalman filter of one long-crested deep-water component and a mean level.
Its state is the amplitude, the phase at a reference point that moves with the newest sample, the angular frequency, the direction of travel, the mean level in the navigation frame and the bank error.
The level absorbs the navigation height error, as a random walk with the drift rate of the run's own navigation filter; that error is common to the samples of an update, so it is not added to each sample's noise.
Each sample is the navigation height of the antenna minus the vertical height the sensor reports, at the point the estimator assumes; the model of a sample is the level plus the component with its second-order self term (the surface of the oracle sea) plus the tips' lever times the bank error.
The first estimate comes from a grid search over the period and the direction (a linear fit of the amplitude, the phase, the level, its trend and the bank error at each grid point, coarse and then fine) and Gauss-Newton on all parameters, made over the samples of the survey.
The filter then updates at every plan, with each sensor thinned to ten samples per update and innovations beyond four sigmas dropped.
Its process noise on the amplitude and the phase grows when the innovations are larger than predicted (an irregular sea needs it, a regular one does not), and a model noise takes up the sea the model leaves out (a wind sea, the rest of an irregular swell).
While the estimate is not ready a new grid search runs every 10 s over the last 120 s, at most twelve times, and replaces the state when it fits that window better; a filter that drops most samples starts again from a grid search.
The estimate is ready when its one-sigma errors are within 0.15 rad of phase, 8 % of the amplitude, 0.3 s of period and 8 deg of direction, with an amplitude above 5 cm, and it stays ready while the phase's sigma is below twice its limit.
These sigmas are the filter's own: they measure the information it believes it has, not its error, and the error tables show where the two part.
Once ready the planner keeps the estimate, also when its sigmas grow past the limits.
The planner's world is the oracle sea's form (one regular component, the COARE 3.5 log wind, the wave wind of the preset, the second-order surface) with the estimated swell.

The survey: the estimator cannot tell the period from the direction along a straight track, and a crest ride holds one phase of the wave under the aircraft, so the first estimate needs flight across the wave in several directions.
The aircraft flies straight along the wind for 10 s and then one level turn at 10 deg/s before the grid search.
In the closed loop the pilot flies the start's steady leg and then the same level turn as a reference cycle (`survey_entry`, at the leg's airspeed), and takes the wave entry only once the estimate is ready.
A first version made of steady legs 60 deg apart turned hard at each change, against the steady reference's tight heading tolerance, and was dropped after short diagnostic runs.

### Errors in the open loop

The error experiment flies kinematic paths over each sea for 300 s with every sensor set and three seeds (the noise and the sea's phases).
The survey path flies the survey and then a crest ride: the air-relative velocity turns at up to 15 deg/s onto the one that moves with the swell's peak at its phase speed and 7 m/s along its crest, and a feedback holds the local phase of the swell at the aircraft at 0.9 rad on the forward face within about 20 s, with a slow wander of 0.03 rad, 10 m above the local surface (the closed loop's slow ride).
Over the two seas of the closed loop the oracle closed loop's own path is flown as well, which has no survey and enters the ride within seconds of the start.
The errors are measured at the aircraft every second against the truth: the phase is the estimated minus the true linear phase of the dominant swell (the analytic signal of its components within 0.75 to 1.33 of its peak frequency), and the height is the estimated amplitude minus the local amplitude, as a fraction of the swell's amplitude (Hs / 2 of the oracle swell; for an irregular swell, the amplitude of the regular wave with its energy).
The tables give 90th percentiles over the seeds' runs with an estimate and over the 10 s before each time since the start.
The seas are the oracle swell (Hs 3 and 2 m, Tp 8, 10 and 12 s, along, across and against a 6 m/s wind, P63), an irregular long-crested swell (the JONSWAP spectrum of gamma 7 of the simulator's swell, between 0.8 and 1.25 of its peak frequency, a swell far from its storm without the wind sea's tail; this cut is a choice of this pass), the same swell under the wind sea of the simulator's sea history, and the sea history itself (`sim/sea_history.py`: 11 m/s for 48 h, then 6 m/s for 6 h from the same direction or from 90 deg to the side, which leaves a short-crested swell of the simulator's spreading and a wind sea; the paths there assume the wind of the first case).
Limits of the models: the barometer's swell pressure uses the dominant wave's phase speed for every component (exact for the oracle swell, overstated for the shorter components of an irregular one); the tip radars' beam is the bare chip's (M16), which a lens narrows; the forward radar has no attitude error; the lidar's return probability is P135's specular factor alone, and P85's nadir share was measured at 2 to 3 m/s of wind.
